State of art approach: Given f normalized, M = max(f*f). Solve LP maximize sum b subject to b>=0 and conv(f,b) <= M. Then rescale and convex combine. This is a projection-like step onto set {b: f*b <= M}. If M is peak of f*f, the new g may have bigger sum? They maintain normalization. Repeating reduces peak. Projection onto simplex with nonneg and sum S:
Given v (not necessarily nonnegative). We can set v = np.maximum(v, 0). If sum <= S, distribute remainder: v += (S-sum)/n. This is projection if all positive? Actually if v nonnegative and sum < S, projection is v + (S-sum)/n. Good. If sum > S, need threshold tau: max(v-tau,0)=S. We can find tau by sorting v descending. Algorithm:
  if v.sum() <= S: return v + (S - v.sum())/n
  u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
  cssv = np.cumsum(u)
  rho = np.nonzero(u * (np.arange(1,n+1) + 1) > (cssv - S))[0][-1]
  tau = (cssv[rho] - S) / (rho + 1)
  return np.maximum(v - tau, 0)
This is O(n log n) per projection. For n=3000 and 10000 steps =>30M log? 30k*10k=300M maybe okay? Bisection 50*3000*10k=1.5B vector ops, worse. Sorting may be faster in C? `np.sort` 30M elements total over 10k? Actually 10k*3000 log 3000 ~300M compares in C, okay maybe few seconds? But memory sort each iter heavy. Bisection with 30 iterations vectorized might be okay too. We can optimize: Since v remains close to simplex, sum difference small. We can use the simple formula if sum within tolerance: if sum > S, subtract proportional? But proportional may create negatives. We can do:
  s = v.sum()
  if s > 0:
     v = v * S / s (scale) ; then clip negatives? Scaling nonnegative if v nonnegative. But after gradient step, v may have negatives. We can first `v = np.maximum(v, 0)`, then if sum <=0, uniform. Then scale to sum S. This is not Euclidean projection; it's multiplicative projection. It may be less stable but faster (no sort). For nonnegative, scaling preserves relative ratios, may work. Previous used Euclidean projection. Which is better? Multiplicative projection onto sum simplex but not nearest; can cause zeros. Could be okay for optimization? We can use a hybrid: after gradient step, clip to 0, then if sum != S, scale by S/sum. This is O(n) and preserves positivity. It is like projective stochastic gradient; may be fine. However Euclidean projection better for convergence. We can implement efficient projection with bisection only when sum > S after clipping; if sum < S, add uniform. Many steps sum may be >S. Bisection 40 iterations. For n=3000, 40*3000=120k per iter; 10k =>1.2B simple ops in C maybe 1s? Actually numpy vector ops overhead 40*10k=400k allocations of length 3000 -> overhead high (maybe 10s). Sort one allocation but C. We can use sorting when needed. Maybe implement fast threshold using `np.partition`? To find tau, need top rho. Could use sort. n=3000 sort maybe ~0.0002s? 10k ->2s. Okay.

Could we cross over to exploit actual score with a general solver using black-box and no assumptions? For hidden nontrivial, local structure. We can use *evolutionary local search* with actual score only. Let's design a robust black-box mutator:
- Maintain best array `best` length n.
- Generate trial by adding a random *smooth Gaussian bump* or *comb* with small amplitude, then project. Since objective depends on overlaps, adding windows can reduce peaks. Use many random positions/widths/amplitudes. Accept if score improves. This is like stochastic local search. For 3000 dims, we can do thousands evals. Each trial conv O(n^2). If 0.02s, 10000=200s. Good.
- Use adaptive step size: if accept, increase step slightly; if reject, decrease. This is a form of ADAPTIVE PATTERN SEARCH. It doesn't need gradients and respects actual objective. It may improve from previous by local perturbations.
- Perturbations: Instead of full random vector (too many dims, likely reject), use low-dimensional smooth directions:
   * Gaussian bump: delta = sigma * exp(-(i-c)^2/(2w^2)) with sigma positive or negative. But negative can create zeros; after projection okay. This can raise/raise troughs.
   * Sinusoidal mode: sigma * cos(2π m i/n + phase)
   * Chirp: sigma * cos(2π (i^2)/(2n) + phase)
   * Random sparse spikes: at few positions add +/- small.
   * Random block scaling: select block, multiply by (1+eps) then normalize.
  This explores local smooth variations.
- Use simulated annealing? Accept occasional worse to escape? Could help. But final must be best; we can keep best. Accept a worse trial with probability exp(-delta/T) to move? Since we need search, maybe. But if actual score not smooth? We can use.

This black-box search might find improvements beyond PGD if actual objective complex/inconsistent with surrogate. It is simple and robust.

Combine PGD and black-box: PGD for many small moves; black-box for occasional larger moves.

- Return.

`def normalize_sum(arr, S):`
  arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
  arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
  arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
  s = arr.sum()
  if s <= 0: return np.full_like(arr, S/len(arr))
  return arr * (S/s)  # multiplicative for initial normalization; for projection use simplex_project.

`def simplex_project(v, S):`
  v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
  v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
  n = v.size
  if n == 0: return v
  # First clip negatives to zero for projection? Euclidean projection should handle negatives by threshold, no need clip? Standard algorithm with u sorted works for any v if we solve max(v-tau,0)=S. If sum of clipped? Use general.
  u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
  cssu = np.cumsum(u)
  # find rho where u*(1:n) > cssu - S
  idx = np.nonzero(u * (np.arange(1, n+1) + 1) > (cssu - S))[0]
  if len(idx)==0: return np.zeros_like(v)
  rho = idx[-1]
  tau = (cssu[rho] - S) / (rho + 1)
  return np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)

```
def simplex_project(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.minimum(np.maximum(v, 0.0), 1e12)
    s = v.sum()
    if s < 1e-15: return np.full(v.shape, S/v.size)
    if s <= S:
       return v + (S - s)/v.size
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u * (np.arange(1,n+1) + 1) > (css - S))[0][-1]
    tau=(css[rho]-S)/(rho+1)
    res=np.maximum(v-tau,0.0)
    # fix rounding
    diff=S-res.sum()
    if diff!=0:
       idx=res.argmax(); res[idx]+=diff
    return res
```

```
def simplex_project(v, S):
  v=np.asarray(v,float)
  n=v.size
  u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
  css=np.cumsum(u)
  rng=np.arange(1,n+1)
  cond = u* (rng+1) > css - S  # condition for rho
  idx=np.nonzero(cond)[0]
  if len(idx)==0:
      # if S <=0? S positive. fallback uniform
      return np.full(n, S/n)
  rho=idx[-1]
  tau=(css[rho]-S)/(rho+1)
  return np.maximum(v-tau, 0.0)
```

`def simplex_project(v,S):`
  v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float)
  n = v.size
  if n == 0: return v
  # If all nan, handle
  if not np.isfinite(v).all(): v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
  s0 = v.sum()
  # Quick cases:
  if S <= 0: return np.zeros_like(v)
  # If v max very small? Sort handles.
  u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
  css = np.cumsum(u)
  k = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
  cond = u * k > (css - S)
  if np.any(cond):
      rho = int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
      tau = (css[rho-1] - S) / rho  # if rho is count? If idx last is 0-based j, count=j+1. Let's be careful.
```
If idx array contains 0-based positions. `rho = idx[-1] + 1`; tau = (css[idx[-1]] - S) / rho.
  res = np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)
  # fix sum
  diff = S - res.sum()
  if diff > 0:
      # add to largest positive? or all? We can distribute to first nonzero/largest v.
      res += diff / n  # might make sum exact but if adds to zeros okay; if due to rounding small.
  elif diff < 0:
      # subtract from largest positive
      idxs = np.argsort(v)[::-1]
      for i in idxs:
          take = min(res[i], -diff); res[i]-=take; diff+=take; if diff==0 break
  return res
```
Adding diff/n if positive small okay. But if diff large due to condition failure, not.
If no cond: use bisection.

Check condition with 0-based: `css[i] - S` and count i+1. cond `u[i]*(i+1) > css[i] - S`. If true, count may be i+1. So `cnt = idx+1`. tau=(css[idx]-S)/cnt.

If cond false for all, e.g., u[0]*1 <= u0 - S -> S <=0? Since S>0, u*1 > u - S always because S>0. So first cond always true? u0 > u0 - S true. So idx not empty. Good.

Thus projection works.

`def soft_grad(c, a, w, tau):`
  z = w * c
  m = z.max()
  e = np.exp((z-m)/tau)
  s = e.sum()
  if s ==0 or not finite: return ... fallback
  p=e/s
  r=p*w
  grad=2*np.correlate(r,a,'valid') - r[0::2]*a
  L=tau*(m+np.log(s))
  return grad, L

For static full weights, we can avoid w array? Keep generic.

Memory: w length 2n-1.

Dynamic topk weights update:
```
def update_top_weights(c, n, base, topk, center_weight):
  w = np.full(len(c), base)
  c_work = c.copy()
  if center_weight is not None: c_work[n-1]=0? no, weight separate.
  # set weight 1 for topk indices of c, symmetric
  idx = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:max(1,2*topk)]
  w[idx]=1.0
  w[n-1]=center_weight if center_weight is not None else base
  w = 0.5*(w + w[::-1])
  return w
```

`def make_gaussian_bump(n, rng, center=None, width=None, amp=None):`
  if center None: center=rng.integers(0,n)
  if width None: width=float(rng.uniform(max(5, n*0.01), max(20,n*0.25)))
  # To keep bounded, if center near edges use two-sided? Gaussian naturally.
  i = np.arange(n)
  delta = amp * np.exp(-0.5*((i-center)/width)**2)
  return delta
But creating i and exp for each trial O(n) and memory; 10000*3000=30M exp, okay but maybe 1s? Actually exp 30M ~0.2s? okay.

For speed, precompute index array `idx = np.arange(n)`. Use it globally? Helper functions top-level, can pass.

Sinusoid: `amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*m*idx/n + phase)`.
Chirp: `amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*(idx*idx)/(2*n) + phase)`.
Block: delta = zeros; delta[start:end] = amp * rng.uniform(-1,1,size=length). Project.
Sparse: delta zeros; delta[positions] += amp * signs.

To maintain diversity, choose method by rng.

import numpy as np, time, math

def eval_seq(arr):
    try:
       lst = [float(x) for x in arr]
       return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
       return float('inf')

def simplex_project(v,S): ...

def soft_grad(c,a,w,tau): ...

def make_weights(n, mode, topk=0, base=0.1, center=None):
    m=2*n-1; w=np.ones(m); c=n-1
    if mode=='full': w.fill(1)
    elif mode=='side': w.fill(1); w[c]=0.0
    elif mode=='center01': w.fill(1); w[c]=0.1
    elif mode=='center001': w[c]=0.01
    elif mode=='topk':
       w.fill(base)
       # initial top by max? We don't have c. For initial, maybe center? Use default all base and a few random? Better use c in run to update. For static not.
    return w

def update_top_weights(c,n,topk,base,center):
    w=np.full_like(c, base)
    if topk>0:
       # take top 2*topk of c, but if center weight should low? let's include all then center override
       idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
       take=idx[:min(len(idx), max(2, 2*topk))]
       w[take]=1.0
    w[n-1]=center if center is not None else base
    w=0.5*(w+w[::-1])
    return w
```
If `base` very small, weights mask positive except if base=0. Use base>0.

`soft_grad`:
```
def soft_grad(c,a,w,tau):
   mask = w > 0
   if not mask.any(): fallback uniform weight
   zc=np.full(c.shape,-np.inf)
   vals = w[mask]*c[mask]
   m = vals.max()
   e=np.zeros(c.shape)
   e[mask]=np.exp((vals-m)/tau)
   s=e.sum()
   if not finite or s<=0: ...
   p=e/s
   r=p*w
   r=0.5*(r+r[::-1])
   g=2*np.correlate(r,a,'valid') - r[0::2]*a
   L=tau*(m+np.log(s))
   return g,L
```
If w full ones, mask all. If vals all -inf? no.

Previous used 1.0. To avoid large divergence, use `lr0=0.5` for side? Config can specify. In PGD, if `lr*max(abs(grad))` too large, projection correct but step can be too big. We can cap effective step: after `gmax = np.max(np.abs(grad))`, if `lr*gmax > 0.5*S?` Actually step elements maybe up to lr*gmax. Uniform elements ~S/n. A step of 1 may move to different shape. Maybe okay. Cap `lr_eff = min(lr, 0.1 / max(gmax,1e-8))`? This makes max step 0.1 (absolute). But if a elements ~0.025, 0.1 huge. Cap 0.05? Better: `max_delta = 0.2 * max(S/n, 1.0)`? S/n=0.025. 0.2*S/n=0.005 too small. Need think.

Gradient magnitudes: For tau small center peak, grad at positions near peak ~2*a ~0.05. lr=1 moves 0.05, comparable to maximum element (if uniform 0.025), can zero out. Maybe okay but aggressive. For uniform full tau large, grad~0.013, lr=1 small. If we cap by gmax, different.

Use heuristic: `step_scale = lr * min(1.0, (0.1 * S) / max(np.abs(grad).sum(), 1e-12))`? This caps L1 change of a to 0.1*S (10% of sum). Since projection preserves sum, L1 of change maybe. Could be stable. But computing sum abs each iteration O(n). Fine. Or use max. Maybe no cap; previous worked.

Let's keep no cap but lr_max bound. If sequence collapses, score bad and revert.

Could use `np.correlate` with 'valid' if r length 2n-1 and a length n. If n=1, r length1, correlate valid length1? r[0::2] length1. c length1. Works.

Baseline generation functions:
```
def init_uniform(n): return np.ones(n, float)
def init_chirp(n, seed, amp=0.7):
  rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
  i=np.arange(n)
  phi=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
  a=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*(i*i)/(2.0*n)+phi)
  return np.maximum(a,1e-6)
def init_hann(n): return np.hanning(n)
def init_blackman(n): return np.blackman(n)
def init_gauss(n, sigma_frac): x=np.linspace(-3,3,n); a=np.exp(-0.5*(x/sigma_frac)**2); +eps
def init_random_positive(n, seed): return np.random.default_rng(seed).exponential(1.0, n)
def init_sparse(n,k,seed): a=zeros; idx=rng.choice(n,k,replace=False); a[idx]=rng.exponential(1,size=k); if sum 0...
```

    def add_array(arr, name): normalize, eval, append if finite
    add prev, uniform, chirps,...
    # also evaluate other lengths? Maybe skip for simplicity.
    best_arr = None; best_score=inf
    for arr,score in candidates: if score<best: ...
    if best_score inf: best_arr=uniform(n); best_score=eval
    # print baseline

      dict(mode='full', tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.05, lr0=1.0, frac=0.20, topk=0),

When interpolating previous to n:
```
def interpolate_seq(arr, n):
   m=arr.size
   if m==n: return arr.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   return np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
```

Implement mutation methods efficiently:
```
def add_gaussian(best, n, S, amp, rng, idx):
  center = rng.integers(0,n)
  width = float(rng.uniform(max(3,0.01*n), max(10,0.25*n)))
  delta = amp * np.exp(-0.5*((idx-center)/width)**2)
  if rng.random()<0.5: delta=-delta
  return delta
```
Creating exp each trial. Could precompute base `idx`. Local variable.

```

`soft_grad` implementation detail for memory:
```
def soft_grad(c, a, w, tau):
    if tau <= 0: tau = 1e-6
    mask = w > 0.0
    if not mask.any():
        # fallback: minimize sum squares? grad =2a? But not used.
        return 2.0*a, 0.0
    vals = w[mask] * c[mask]
    m = float(np.max(vals))
    e_full = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
    exp_vals = np.exp((vals - m) / tau)
    s = float(np.sum(exp_vals))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a), m*tau
    e_full[mask] = exp_vals
    p = e_full / s
    r = p * w
    r = 0.5 * (r + r[::-1])
    g = 2.0 * np.correlate(r, a, 'valid') - r[0::2] * a
    L = tau * (m + np.log(s))
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return g, L
```
For full weights all ones, mask length 5999, vals=c, exp etc. Good. For `w` with base positive, mask all. Could optimize by if all `w>0`, no zc full? We still allocate e_full. okay.

If `w` has zero center, mask excludes center; `vals` length 2n-2. `e_full` zeros at center. Good.

`make_weights(n, mode, center=None)`:
```
w = np.ones(2*n-1)
if mode == 'side': if center is None: w[n-1]=0 else w[n-1]=center
elif mode.startswith('center'): w[n-1]=float(mode.split('_')[1])? Simpler pass center.
```
In configs, pass mode and center. `make_weights(n, base=1.0, center=1.0)` maybe.
```
def make_weights(n, center=1.0, base=1.0):
   w = np.full(2*n-1, base)
   w[n-1]=center
   w=0.5*(w+w[::-1])
   return w
```
For full center=1 base=1, side center=0 base=1, center001 center=0.01 base=1.

`update_top_weights(c,n,topk,base,center)`:
```
w = np.full(c.shape, base)
if topk > 0:
   k = int(min(len(c), max(2, 2*topk)))
   idx = np.argpartition(c, len(c)-k)[len(c)-k:]  # top k indices unsorted, faster
   # to be safe if k small: idx = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:k]
   w[idx] = 1.0
   # include symmetric? w symmetric later; if idx not symmetric, sym will complete.
w[n-1] = center if center is not None else base
w = 0.5*(w + w[::-1])
# If center should be <= base? center can be 0.0.
return w
```

def build_initials(n, prev_arr, seed):
   arrs=[]
   arrs.append(np.ones(n))
   if prev_arr is not None: arrs.append(interpolate_seq(prev_arr,n))

Let's define `run_smooth` final:
```
def run_smooth(a0, mode, tau_hi, tau_lo, lr0, budget, actual_every=120, topk=0, base=0.1, center=0.0, rng=None, verbose=False):
    if rng is None: rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    n = int(a0.size); S = float(np.sqrt(2.0*n))
    a = simplex_project(np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64), S)
    best = a.copy(); best_score = eval_seq(best)
    # initial weights
    if mode == 'topk':
       c0 = np.convolve(a,a)
       w = update_top_weights(c0,n,topk,base,center)
    else:
       w = make_weights(n, center=center if center is not None else 1.0, base=1.0)
    lr = float(lr0); lr_min = max(1e-3, lr0*0.05); lr_max = lr0*10.0
    step=0; patience=0; bad=0; t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(1e-6,budget)
    dyn_interval=100
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       elapsed = time.monotonic()-t0; frac = min(1.0, elapsed/max(budget,1e-6))
       if tau_hi > 0 and tau_lo > 0:
          tau = tau_hi * (tau_lo/tau_hi) ** frac
       else: tau=1.0
       c = np.convolve(a,a)
       if mode == 'topk' and step % dyn_interval == 0:
          w = update_top_weights(c,n,topk,base,center)
       grad,L = soft_grad(c,a,w,tau)
       a_new = simplex_project(a - lr*grad, S)
       if patience > 3:
          noise = (0.02*S/n)*rng.standard_normal(n)
          a_new = simplex_project(a_new + noise, S)
       a = a_new; step += 1
       if step % actual_every == 0:
          sc = eval_seq(a)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
             best_score = sc; best = a.copy(); patience=0; bad=0
             if lr < lr_max: lr *= 1.03
          else:
             bad += 1
             if bad >= 2:
                if lr > lr_min: lr *= 0.85
             patience += 1
             if np.isfinite(sc) and sc > best_score*1.5 + 0.2:
                a = best.copy(); lr = max(lr_min, lr*0.5); patience=0; bad=0
             elif patience > 20:
                a = simplex_project(best + (0.05*S/n)*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
                patience=0; bad=0; lr = max(lr_min, lr0*0.5)
       if verbose and step % 500 == 0:
          print(f"      smooth {mode} step={step} tau={tau:.4f} lr={lr:.3f} best={best_score:.6f}")
    # final eval of best and a maybe
    sc_a = eval_seq(a)
    if sc_a < best_score: best_score, best = sc_a, a.copy()
    return best, best_score
```
If budget small, actual check may not occur; final eval a. Good.

Potential issue: `run_smooth` best_score initial eval every call can be costly but okay. If many calls, baseline already. But okay.

def mutation_search(a_init, budget, rng, actual_every=None):
    n=a_init.size; S=float(np.sqrt(2.0*n))
    a = simplex_project(a_init, S)
    best = a.copy(); best_score = eval_seq(best)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    quad = (idx*idx)/(2.0*n)
    mean = S/n
    amp = max(0.1*mean, 1e-6); max_amp=5.0*mean; min_amp=1e-5*mean? no.
    deadline=...
    no=0
    while time < deadline:
       trial = best.copy()
       r = rng.random()
       if r < 0.30:
          center = int(rng.integers(0,n))
          width = float(rng.uniform(max(2.0,0.005*n), max(5.0,0.2*n)))
          delta = amp * np.exp(-0.5*((idx-center)/width)**2)
          if rng.random()<0.5: delta=-delta
          trial += delta
       elif r < 0.50:
           m = int(rng.integers(1, 21))
           phase = float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
           trial += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*m*idx/n + phase)
       elif r < 0.70:
           phase = float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
           trial += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*quad + phase)
       elif r < 0.85:
           L = int(rng.integers(10, max(20, n//10)))
           start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-L)))
           trial[start:start+L] += amp * rng.standard_normal(L)
       else:
           k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//200)))
           pos = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
           trial[pos] += (amp*10.0) * rng.choice([-1.0,1.0], size=k)
       trial = simplex_project(trial, S)
       sc = eval_seq(trial)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=sc; best=trial.copy(); no=0; amp=min(max_amp, amp*1.1)
       else:
          no +=1
          if no > 10:
             amp=max(min_amp, amp*0.9); no=0
          if no > 300: # big jump
             trial = simplex_project(best + (2.0*mean)*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
             sc2 = eval_seq(trial)
             if sc2 < best_score: update
             else: amp = max(min_amp, amp*0.7); no=0
    return best,best_score
```

Define:
```
def fast_project(v,S):
    v = np.asarray(v,float)
    v = np.maximum(v,0.0)
    s = v.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.full(v.shape, S/v.size)
    return v * (S/s)
```
This is multiplicative; if a trial has negatives, clipping them reduces their influence. Good for random local search. Use in mutation and maybe perturb. PGD uses `simplex_project` for convergence.

However, if trial has large negative, clipping+scaling may create uniform-ish; okay.

Use `fast_project` for big jumps too.

In `run_smooth` loop:
```
prev_proxy = 2*n*np.convolve(a,a).max()/S2
best_proxy=prev_proxy; best=a.copy()
while...:
  c = np.convolve(a,a)  # at current a
  cmax = c.max()
  proxy = 2*n*cmax/S2
  if proxy < best_proxy: best_proxy, best = proxy, a.copy()
  # lr adapt using last_proxy:

Detailed `run_smooth`:
```
def run_smooth(a0, mode='full', tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.05, lr0=1.0, budget=60.0,
               topk=0, base=0.1, alpha=1.0, rng=None, verbose=False):
    if rng is None: rng = np.random.default_rng(123)
    n = a0.size
    S = np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a = simplex_project(a0, S)
    best = a.copy()
    c = np.convolve(a,a)
    best_score = float(np.max(c))
    last_score = best_score
    adapt_last = best_score
    lr = float(lr0); lr_min = max(1e-3, lr0*0.01); lr_max = lr0*20.0
    no_improve=0
    # mask for topk
    if mode in ('topk','topk_var'):
       mask = update_top_weights(c,n,topk,base)
    else: mask=None
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(1e-6,budget)
    step=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0; frac=min(1.0, elapsed/max(budget,1e-6))
       tau = tau_hi * (tau_lo/tau_hi)**frac
       # c for current a already computed; but after first iteration we update a; we can recompute at top.
       c = np.convolve(a,a)
       score = float(np.max(c))
       if score < best_score:
           best_score = score; best = a.copy(); no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve += 1
       if step % 5 == 0:
          if score < adapt_last - 1e-10:
              lr = min(lr_max, lr*1.03)
          else:
              lr = max(lr_min, lr*0.92)
          adapt_last = score
       if no_improve > 5000:
          a = simplex_project(best + (0.02*S/n)*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
          no_improve=0; lr=max(lr_min, lr0*0.5); adapt_last=np.inf
          continue
       if mask is not None and step % 100 == 0:
          mask = update_top_weights(c,n,topk,base)
       w = compute_weights(c, mode, tau, alpha, mask, n)
       g = 2.0*np.correlate(w,a,'valid')
       if not finite g: break
       a = simplex_project(a - lr*g, S)
       step +=1
       if verbose and step%1000==0: print(...)
    # final check a
    c=np.convolve(a,a); sc=float(np.max(c))
    if sc < best_score: best_score=sc; best=a.copy()
    return best, best_score
```
Need avoid recomputing c twice: At top we compute c for score and weights. After update, next loop. Good. First loop computes initial c; after step, next. But at end we compute final a. Fine.

`compute_weights(c, mode, tau, alpha, mask, n)`:
- mode 'full': `p = softmax(c, tau)` (no weights)
- 'var': `p = softmax(c, tau); cm=c.mean(); w=p + alpha*(c - cm); w=np.clip(w,-1,1); scale=max(abs(w).max(),1e-6); return w/scale` (if scale). But if we scale, alpha effectively changes. Could return raw if scale not too big? To maintain gradient scale, scale. Use scaling.
- 'topk': `p=softmax; w=p*mask;` (positive). `mask` may have base >0.
- 'topk_var': `p=softmax; cm=c.mean(); w=p*mask + alpha*(c-cm)*mask?` Maybe use full variance even if mask? Could. Let's define `'var_full'` for variance without mask.

We can use `mask` only for topk. If mode full/var mask None.

`softmax(c, tau)` helper:
```
def softmax_conv(c, tau):
    m = np.max(c)
    e = np.exp((c - m)/tau)
    s = e.sum()
    if s ==0 or not finite: return np.full(c.size, 1.0/c.size)
    return e/s
```

Now, we should ensure `simplex_project` with correct standard algorithm. Let's implement carefully.

```
def simplex_project(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v
    if S <= 0: return np.zeros(n)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    k = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * k > (css - S)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, S/n, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1]
    cnt = idx + 1
    tau = (css[idx] - S) / cnt
    res = np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)
    # correct sum
    diff = S - res.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12 * max(1.0, S):
       if diff > 0:
          # distribute to entries with largest v
          idxs = np.argsort(v)[::-1]
          for i in idxs:
             add = min(diff, max(0.0, ?)) # adding to any may break threshold? just add all to largest
             res[i] += diff; break
       else:
          idxs = np.argsort(v)[::-1]
          for i in idxs:
             take = min(res[i], -diff)
             res[i] -= take
             diff += take
             if diff >= -1e-15: break
    return res
```
Adding all diff to largest may break nonneg? diff small. If diff positive and largest res maybe zero? if v all negative, tau negative res positive; okay. If diff negative, subtract from largest positive. Good.

Could use bisection fallback if projection weird. But standard should work.

`fast_project`:
```
def fast_project(v,S):
   v = np.asarray(v,float)
   v = np.maximum(np.nan_to_num(v, 0,0,0), 0.0)
   s = v.sum()
   if s <= 1e-15: return np.full(v.shape, S/v.size)
   return v * (S/s)
```

Now, `build_initials` normalized to S = sqrt(2n). Need ensure sum >0.01. For n large S >14.

Functions:
```
def chirp_init(n, seed, amp):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    phase = float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
    a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*x*x + phase)
    return np.maximum(a, 1e-3)

def sine_init(n, seed, freq, amp):
    rng...
    a=1+amp*np.cos(2π*freq*i/n+phase); return max(a,1e-3)

def gaussian_window(n, sigma): x=np.linspace(-4,4,n); return np.exp(-0.5*(x/sigma)**2)+1e-3
```
Maybe include spectral flatness: `a = np.abs(np.exp(1j*chirp) + 0.5)`? This is 1+0.5 sin, okay.

```
def sanitize_seq(arr):
  arr = np.asarray(arr, float)
  arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
  arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
  arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
  if arr.sum() <= 0.01: arr = np.ones(arr.size)*1.0
  return arr
```
But if values capped to 1000, sum may change; score scale invariant if uniform cap? If one spike >1000, cap changes. Our sequences S<1000, no cap. If sanitize after final sum S maybe >1000? n not huge. okay.

`eval_seq`:
```
def eval_seq(arr):
  try:
     a = sanitize_seq(np.asarray(arr, float).ravel())
     return float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
  except Exception:
     return float('inf')
```

`proxy_score` should not sanitize cap? Use raw but if a has negative, max? We ensure nonnegative. For general:
```
def proxy_score(arr):
   a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
   a=np.maximum(np.nan_to_num(a,0,0,0),0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <=0: return inf
   c=np.convolve(a,a)
   return float(2*a.size*float(np.max(c))/(s*s))
```
For normalized S=sqrt(2n), equals maxc.

Sorting 3000 each iter maybe okay. For 50k iter, sort 50k*3000log ~300M C ops, maybe 1-2s? Actually np.sort 3000 ~ 50us, 50k=2.5s. Good. Vector operations correlate dominate.

But `simplex_project` creates sort array and cumsum each time. Fine.

```
import numpy as np, time

# helpers...

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    prev = get_prev_sequence()
    # Determine primary length
    lengths = [3000]
    if prev is not None and 100 <= prev.size <= 10000:
        lengths.insert(0, int(prev.size))
    lengths += [4000, 5000]
    # unique limited
    unique_lengths=[]
    for L in lengths:
       if L>0 and L not in unique_lengths: unique_lengths.append(L)
    # Quick length selection using uniform and prev
    global_best=None; global_score=np.inf; global_n=None
    for n in unique_lengths:
       S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
       a=normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n), S)
       sc=proxy_score(a)
       if sc < global_score: global_score=sc; global_best=a; global_n=n
       if prev is not None:
          a2=normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(prev,n), S)
          sc2=proxy_score(a2)
          if sc2 < global_score: ...
       # Maybe chirp small? no to save
    n = global_n if global_n else 3000
    S = np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    # Build initials
    initials = build_initials(n, prev, seed)
    # Add interpolated small warm start later maybe
    scored=[]
    for a in initials:
       a=normalize_to_sum(a,S)
       if a.size != n: continue
       sc=proxy_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc,a))
    scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])  # lambda not allowed? Rule no lambda functions. Need avoid lambda. Use loop or `scored.sort()` works if tuples first element score. Yes tuple sort no lambda.
    # best initial
    if scored: best_init=scored[0][1] else uniform
    best_arrays = [a for _,a in scored[:3]]
    # actual best init
    best_actual = best_init.copy(); best_actual_score = eval_seq(best_actual)
    work = best_init.copy(); proxy_best=best_actual_score? no, proxy score = scored[0][0]
    print baseline.
```

Now, implement `run_smooth` with mode 'var' and 'topk'. Need parameter `alpha` for var. `compute_weights` top-level:
```
def compute_weights(c, mode, tau, alpha, mask, n):
    p = softmax_conv(c, tau)
    if mode == 'full': return p
    if mode == 'var':
       cm = float(np.mean(c))
       w = p + (alpha / (2.0 * n)) * (c - cm)
       # optional clip to avoid huge? c-cm O n? Could be large if spikes. Clip to [-1,1]? If clip O1 and divide by n? We scaled alpha/n, so w O1/n unless c-cm huge >n. If spike, c-m could be S^2 huge? But sum fixed S=sqrt(2n), max coefficient can be up to S^2=2n if one spike, c-cm O n. alpha/n * n = O1. Gradient too large. Clip to [-1,1] then weight O1, maybe large. Better scale by current cmax? Use `var = (c - cm) / (np.max(c) + 1e-6)` so O[ -1,1]. w = p + alpha * var? But p O1/n. If alpha=0.01, var weight O0.01, gradient O S? wait correlate of O0.01 with a window sum S~sqrt(n) =>0.01*77=0.77, too large. Need w O1/n. So set `w = p + (alpha / n) * var`. Then O1/n. Good.
       var = (c - cm) / (float(np.max(c)) + 1e-6)
       w = p + (alpha / float(n)) * var
       return w
    if mode == 'topk':
       if mask is None: return p
       return p * mask
    if mode == 'topk_var':
       ...
    return p
```
For var, `var` in [-1,1], alpha/n ~1e-4 for n=3000; p average 1/(6000)=1.7e-4. alpha=1 comparable. Good. If spike cmax large, var normalized, weight O1/n. Good. Use `alpha` param maybe 1,3,5. No scaling clip needed. If c has zeros, var -1, weight slightly negative. Good.

`softmax_conv`: c all finite? c from nonnegative finite. If tau <=0, set small.
```
def softmax_conv(c, tau):
    if tau <= 0: tau = 1e-6
    m = float(np.max(c))
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       return np.full(c.size, 1.0/c.size)
    return e / s
```

```
def get_prev_sequence():
    val = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if val is None: return None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(val, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.size ==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    if arr.sum() <= 1e-12: return None
    return arr
```

`interpolate_seq`:
```
def interpolate_seq(arr, n):
    m=arr.size
    if m == n: return arr.astype(float).copy()
    if m == 1: return np.full(n, arr[0])
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
```

`normalize_to_sum`:
```
def normalize_to_sum(arr, S):
    a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size, S/a.size)
    return a*(S/s)
```
If a.size 0? return empty? We won't.

`two_bump_init`:
```
def two_bump_init(n, centers, width):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    a=np.zeros(n)
    for c in centers:
        a += np.exp(-0.5*((x-c)/width)**2)
    return a + 1e-3
```

Implement `correlate_weighted(w, a)`:
```
def correlate_weighted(w, a):
    # if dense or nnz large:
    nnz = int(np.count_nonzero(w))
    if nnz <= 200:
       idx = np.nonzero(w)[0]
       res = np.zeros(a.size)
       val = w[idx]
       # H_j = sum_i a_i w_{i+j}; equivalently for each k with w_k, add w_k * a_{k-j} for valid j.
       # For each k, j ranges max(0,k-n+1) to min(n-1,k), a_i where i=k-j -> j=k-i for i=0..n-1.
       # So res[j] += w_k * a[i] where j = k - i, 0<=j<n.
       # Loop over k (nnz small): i_start=max(0,k-n+1), i_end=min(n-1,k). j = k - i, so j from k-i_end to k-i_start.
       for kk, ww in zip(idx, val):
           i0 = max(0, kk - a.size + 1)
           i1 = min(a.size - 1, kk)
           # res[j0:j1+1] += ww * a[i1:i0:-1]? Need correct slice.
           # j = kk - i. When i=i0, j=kk-i0; when i=i1, j=kk-i1.
           # i increasing => j decreasing. Use a slice i0..i1+1 reversed.
           j0 = kk - i1
           j1 = kk - i0
           # res[j0:j1+1] += ww * a[i0:i1+1][::-1]
           res[j0:j1+1] += ww * a[i0:i1+1][::-1]
       return res
    else:
       return np.correlate(w, a, 'valid')
```
Check n=3,k=2,w p2: i0=0,i1=2; j0=0,j1=2; res[0:3]+=w*a[::-1] = [a2,a1,a0]. H_j for k=2: j=0 i=2 a2, j=1 i=1 a1, j=2 i=0 a0. correct.
For k=1: i0=0,i1=1; j0=0,j1=1? kk-i1=0, kk-i0=1; a[0:2][::-1]=[a1,a0]; res[0]+=w*a1 (j0 i1? Wait H_j sum_i a_i w_{i+j}=w_{?}. For fixed k=1, H_j includes i=k-j: j=0 i=1 a1; j=1 i=0 a0. res[0]=a1, res[1]=a0. Our j0=0 gets a[i1]=a1, j1=1 gets a[i0]=a0. Good.
Thus sparse correlation works.

```python
"""
Minimize 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2 for non-negative sequences.

The code uses a corrected soft-max/polynomial-convolution gradient,
variance-regularized flattening, active top-peak optimization, black-box
smooth mutations, and gradient line-search polishing.  All optimizer scoring
uses the same quadratic proxy as the evaluator after normalization, so no
expensive evaluator calls are needed inside the inner loops.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def sanitize_sequence(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.01:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a


def eval_sequence_safe(arr):
    try:
        a = sanitize_sequence(arr)
        return float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def proxy_score(arr):
    """Exact proxy for the evaluator on non-negative, un-capped sequences."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return float("inf")
    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    return float(2.0 * float(a.size) * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))


def normalize_to_sum(arr, S):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([S], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, S / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (S / s)


def fast_project(v, S):
    """Fast multiplicative projection to non-negative vectors with sum S."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(v.shape, S / float(v.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return v * (S / s)


def simplex_project(v, S):
    """
    Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = S}.
    Uses the standard sorted-vector algorithm.
    """
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if S <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    k = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * k > (css - S)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    idx = int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
    cnt = idx + 1
    tau = (css[idx] - S) / float(cnt)
    res = np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)

    diff = S - float(np.sum(res))
    tol = 1e-12 * max(1.0, S)
    if abs(diff) > tol:
        order = np.argsort(v)[::-1]
        if diff > 0:
            res[order[0]] += diff
        else:
            rem = -diff
            for i in order:
                if rem <= 0.0:
                    break
                take = min(res[i], rem)
                res[i] -= take
                rem -= take
    return res


def get_prev_sequence():
    val = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if val is None:
        return None
    try:
        a = np.asarray(val, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return a


def interpolate_seq(arr, n):
    m = int(arr.size)
    if m == n:
        return np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(arr[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64))


def symmetrize_seq(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    return 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])


def chirp_init(n, seed, amp):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    a = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x * x + phase)
    return np.maximum(a, 1e-4)


def sine_init(n, seed, freq, amp):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    a = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * idx / float(n) + phase)
    return np.maximum(a, 1e-4)


def gaussian_window(n, sigma):
    x = np.linspace(-4.0, 4.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.exp(-0.5 * (x / float(sigma)) ** 2) + 1e-4


def two_bump_init(n, centers, width):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    w = float(width)
    for c in centers:
        a += np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - float(c)) / w) ** 2)
    return a + 1e-4


def sparse_init(n, k, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    k = max(1, min(n, int(k)))
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    pos = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
    a[pos] = rng.exponential(1.0, size=k)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.0:
        a[0] = 1.0
    return a


def build_initials(n, prev_arr, seed):
    arrs = []
    arrs.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    if prev_arr is not None:
        arrs.append(interpolate_seq(prev_arr, n))

    # Chirps with different amplitudes/phases.
    for s, amp in [(0, 0.5), (1, 0.8), (2, 1.0)]:
        arrs.append(chirp_init(n, int(seed) + 100 + s, amp))

    # Low-frequency amplitude modulation.
    arrs.append(sine_init(n, int(seed) + 110, 1, 0.5))
    arrs.append(sine_init(n, int(seed) + 111, 3, 0.5))

    # Window shapes.
    arrs.append(np.hanning(n) + 1e-4)
    arrs.append(np.blackman(n) + 1e-4)
    arrs.append(gaussian_window(n, 0.5))
    arrs.append(gaussian_window(n, 1.0))

    # Two-bump shapes.
    arrs.append(two_bump_init(n, [0.25, 0.75], 0.10))
    arrs.append(two_bump_init(n, [0.20, 0.50, 0.80], 0.08))

    # Random positive.
    arrs.append(np.random.default_rng(int(seed) + 120).exponential(1.0, n))

    # Sparse structured sequence.
    arrs.append(sparse_init(n, max(10, n // 3), int(seed) + 121))

    # Symmetric version of the best previous sequence if available.
    if prev_arr is not None:
        p = interpolate_seq(prev_arr, n)
        arrs.append(symmetrize_seq(p))

    return arrs


def softmax_conv(c, tau):
    if tau <= 0.0:
        tau = 1e-6
    m = float(np.max(c))
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def update_top_weights(c, n, npeaks, base):
    m = c.size
    w = np.full(m, float(base), dtype=np.float64)
    if npeaks > 0:
        k = int(min(m, max(1, int(npeaks))))
        if k >= m:
            w.fill(1.0)
        else:
            idx = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:k]
            w[idx] = 1.0
    return w


def compute_weights(c, mode, tau, alpha, mask, n, npeaks, base):
    p = softmax_conv(c, tau)
    if mode == "full":
        return p

    if mode == "var":
        cm = float(np.mean(c))
        scale = float(np.max(c)) + 1e-9
        if scale <= 0.0:
            scale = 1.0
        var = (c - cm) / scale
        w = p + (float(alpha) / float(n)) * var
        return w

    if mode == "topk":
        if mask is None:
            mask = update_top_weights(c, n, npeaks, base)
        w = p * mask
        s = float(np.sum(w))
        if s > 1e-15:
            w = w / s
        else:
            w = p
        return w

    return p


def correlate_weighted(w, a):
    """
    Returns H_j = sum_i a_i * w_{i+j}, j=0..n-1.
    Uses a sparse implementation when w has few nonzeros.
    """
    if w.size != 2 * a.size - 1:
        return np.correlate(w, a, "valid")
    nnz = int(np.count_nonzero(w))
    if nnz == 0:
        return np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    if nnz > 220:
        return np.correlate(w, a, "valid")

    idx = np.nonzero(w)[0]
    vals = w[idx]
    res = np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    for kk, ww in zip(idx, vals):
        kk = int(kk)
        if ww == 0.0:
            continue
        i0 = max(0, kk - n + 1)
        i1 = min(n - 1, kk)
        if i0 > i1:
            continue
        j0 = kk - i1
        j1 = kk - i0
        if j0 < 0 or j1 >= n:
            continue
        res[j0:j1 + 1] += ww * a[i0:i1 + 1][::-1]
    return res


def run_smooth(a0, mode, tau_hi, tau_lo, lr0, budget,
               npeaks=0, base=0.0, alpha=1.0, rng=None, verbose=False):
    """
    Projected gradient descent on a soft-max approximation of max conv(a,a),
    with optional active top-peak or variance regularization.
    """
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(3000, dtype=np.float64), float("inf")

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
    a = simplex_project(a0, S)

    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    best_score = float(np.max(c))
    best = a.copy()

    if mode == "topk" and npeaks <= 0:
        mode = "full"

    mask = None
    if mode == "topk":
        mask = update_top_weights(c, n, npeaks, base)

    lr = float(lr0)
    lr_min = max(1e-3, float(lr0) * 0.01)
    lr_max = float(lr0) * 80.0
    adapt_last = best_score
    no_improve = 0
    step = 0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1e-6, float(budget))

    noise_scale = max(1e-6, 0.012 * S / float(np.sqrt(float(n))))

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        frac = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1e-6, float(budget)))
        if tau_hi > 0.0 and tau_lo > 0.0:
            tau = float(tau_hi) * (float(tau_lo) / float(tau_hi)) ** frac
        else:
            tau = 1.0

        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        score = float(np.max(c))

        if score < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = score
            best = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if step % 5 == 0:
            if score < adapt_last - 1e-10:
                lr = min(lr_max, lr * 1.03)
            else:
                lr = max(lr_min, lr * 0.90)
            adapt_last = score

        if no_improve > 2500:
            a = simplex_project(best + noise_scale * rng.standard_normal(n), S)
            no_improve = 0
            lr = max(lr_min, float(lr0) * 0.5)
            adapt_last = float("inf")
            step += 1
            continue

        if mode == "topk" and step % 100 == 0:
            mask = update_top_weights(c, n, npeaks, base)

        w = compute_weights(c, mode, tau, alpha, mask, n, npeaks, base)
        h = correlate_weighted(w, a)
        g = 2.0 * h

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

        a = simplex_project(a - lr * g, S)
        step += 1

        if verbose and step % 1000 == 0:
            print(f"        smooth {mode} step={step} tau={tau:.5f} "
                  f"lr={lr:.3f} score={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}")

    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    final_score = float(np.max(c))
    if final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
        best = a.copy()
    return best, best_score


def mutation_search(a_init, budget, rng):
    """
    Proxy-score stochastic local search using smooth low-dimensional
    perturbations (bumps, sinusoids, chirps, blocks, spikes).
    """
    a_init = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a_init.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a_init, float("inf")

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
    best = simplex_project(a_init, S)
    best_score = float(np.max(np.convolve(best, best)))

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    quad = (idx * idx) / (2.0 * float(n))
    mean = S / float(n)

    amp = 0.1 * mean
    min_amp = 1e-4 * mean
    max_amp = 5.0 * mean

    trials = 0
    no_improve = 0
    big_counter = 0
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1e-6, float(budget))

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        trial = best.copy()
        r = float(rng.random())

        if r < 0.30:
            center = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            width = float(rng.uniform(max(2.0, 0.005 * n), max(5.0, 0.20 * n)))
            delta = amp * np.exp(-0.5 * ((idx - center) / width) ** 2)
            if rng.random() < 0.5:
                delta = -delta
            trial += delta

        elif r < 0.50:
            m = int(rng.integers(1, 21))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            trial += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * idx / float(n) + phase)

        elif r < 0.70:
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            trial += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * quad + phase)

        elif r < 0.85:
            L = int(rng.integers(10, max(20, n // 10)))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - L)))
            trial[start:start + L] += amp * rng.standard_normal(L)

        else:
            k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 200)))
            pos = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
            trial[pos] += (amp * 10.0) * rng.choice([-1.0, 1.0], size=k)

        trial = fast_project(trial, S)
        sc = float(np.max(np.convolve(trial, trial)))

        trials += 1
        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = sc
            best = trial.copy()
            amp = min(max_amp, amp * 1.10)
            no_improve = 0
            big_counter = 0
            if trials % 5000 == 0:
                print(f"        mutation improve trial={trials} score={sc:.6f}")
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            big_counter += 1
            if no_improve > 20:
                amp = max(min_amp, amp * 0.85)
                no_improve = 0

            if big_counter > 350:
                trial = fast_project(best + (0.35 * mean) * rng.standard_normal(n), S)
                sc2 = float(np.max(np.convolve(trial, trial)))
                if sc2 < best_score:
                    best_score = sc2
                    best = trial.copy()
                    amp = min(max_amp, amp * 1.05)
                else:
                    amp = max(min_amp, amp * 0.70)
                big_counter = 0

                if trials % 10000 == 0:
                    trial2 = fast_project(
                        symmetrize_seq(best) + (0.05 * mean) * rng.standard_normal(n),
                        S
                    )
                    sc3 = float(np.max(np.convolve(trial2, trial2)))
                    if sc3 < best_score:
                        best_score = sc3
                        best = trial2.copy()

    return best, best_score


def alpha_search(a_init, budget, rng):
    """
    Short line search along a few surrogate gradient directions.
    """
    a_init = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a_init.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a_init, float("inf")

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
    mean = S / float(n)
    best = simplex_project(a_init, S)
    best_score = float(np.max(np.convolve(best, best)))

    modes = [
        ("full", 1.0, 1.0, 0, 0.0),
        ("var", 0.5, 2.0, 0, 0.0),
        ("topk", 0.3, 1.0, 20, 0.0),
    ]

    step_fracs = [0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 30.0]
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1e-6, float(budget))

    for mode, tau, alpha, npeaks, base in modes:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break

        c = np.convolve(best, best)
        if mode == "topk" and npeaks <= 0:
            mode_use = "full"
        else:
            mode_use = mode

        mask = None
        if mode_use == "topk":
            mask = update_top_weights(c, n, npeaks, base)

        w = compute_weights(c, mode_use, tau, alpha, mask, n, npeaks, base)
        h = correlate_weighted(w, best)
        g = 2.0 * h
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-14:
            continue

        d = g / gmax
        for f in step_fracs:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                break
            trial = simplex_project(best - float(f) * mean * d, S)
            sc = float(np.max(np.convolve(trial, trial)))
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = sc
                best = trial.copy()

    return best, best_score


def update_actual_best(current_best, current_score, candidate):
    """Return (best, score) using the true evaluator on candidate."""
    if candidate is None:
        return current_best, current_score
    try:
        sc = eval_sequence_safe(candidate)
    except Exception:
        return current_best, current_score
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_score - 1e-14:
        return np.asarray(candidate, dtype=np.float64).copy(), float(sc)
    return current_best, current_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """Search for a low-peak self-convolution non-negative sequence."""
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 35.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    prev = get_prev_sequence()

    # Choose a primary length.  Prefer the provided construction length if it is reasonable.
    lengths = [3000]
    if prev is not None and 100 <= prev.size <= 10000:
        lengths.insert(0, int(prev.size))
    lengths.extend([4000, 5000])

    unique_lengths = []
    for L in lengths:
        L = int(L)
        if L <= 0:
            continue
        if L not in unique_lengths:
            unique_lengths.append(L)

    best_arr = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    best_n = unique_lengths[0]

    # Very quick length scouting with uniform and interpolated previous sequence.
    for n in unique_lengths:
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))

        uni = normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), S)
        sc = proxy_score(uni)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_arr = uni.copy()
            best_n = n

        if prev is not None:
            p_interp = normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(prev, n), S)
            scp = proxy_score(p_interp)
            # Only switch length if it is clearly better than current best.
            if np.isfinite(scp) and (best_arr is None or scp < best_score - 0.005):
                best_score = scp
                best_arr = p_interp.copy()
                best_n = n

    n = int(best_n)
    S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))

    # Ensure a safe default.
    if best_arr is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(best_arr)):
        best_arr = normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), S_base)
        best_n = n

    # Build and score initial candidates at the selected length.
    raw_initials = build_initials(n, prev, seed)
    scored = []
    for i, a0 in enumerate(raw_initials):
        a0 = normalize_to_sum(a0, S_base)
        if a0.size != n:
            continue
        a0 = np.maximum(a0, 0.0)
        if float(np.sum(a0)) <= 0.01:
            continue
        try:
            sc = proxy_score(a0)
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, i, a0))

    scored.sort()

    # Validate a few top candidates with the true evaluator and set the global best.
    for sc, i, a0 in scored[:6]:
        best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, a0)

    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), S_base)
        best_score = eval_sequence_safe(best_arr)

    n = int(best_arr.size)
    S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
    best_arr = normalize_to_sum(best_arr, S_base)

    print(f"initial best: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Symmetric version of the current best can act as a useful restart.
    sym_best = normalize_to_sum(symmetrize_seq(best_arr), S_base)
    best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, sym_best)

    # Small-n warm start: optimize a short sequence, then interpolate it up.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 300:
        small_n = 256
        small_inits = build_initials(small_n, None, seed + 5555)
        small_best = None
        small_score = float("inf")
        S_small = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(small_n)))
        for a0 in small_inits:
            a0 = normalize_to_sum(a0, S_small)
            try:
                sc = proxy_score(a0)
            except Exception:
                sc = float("inf")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < small_score:
                small_score = sc
                small_best = a0

        if small_best is not None:
            small_tb = min(70.0, max(15.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.04))
            small_best, _ = run_smooth(
                small_best, "full", 1.0, 0.05, 1.0, small_tb, rng=rng
            )
            small_tb = min(45.0, max(10.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.03))
            small_best, _ = run_smooth(
                small_best, "var", 0.5, 0.02, 1.0, small_tb,
                alpha=2.0, rng=rng
            )

            warm = normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(small_best, n), S_base)
            best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, warm)
            print(f"after small warm start: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Brief exploration from a few independent bases.
    bases = []
    if len(scored) > 0:
        bases.append(scored[0][2])
    if len(scored) > 1:
        bases.append(scored[1][2])
    bases.append(sym_best)
    if prev is not None:
        bases.append(normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(prev, n), S_base))

    # Remove obviously duplicate bases by score/identity.
    used = []
    clean_bases = []
    for b in bases:
        b = normalize_to_sum(b, S_base)
        dup = False
        for u in used:
            if b.size == u.size and float(np.sqrt(np.sum((b - u) ** 2))) < 1e-3:
                dup = True
                break
        if not dup:
            used.append(b)
            clean_bases.append(b)

    if clean_bases:
        explore_tb_each = min(90.0, max(15.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.07))
        for b in clean_bases[:3]:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 30:
                break
            arr, _ = run_smooth(b, "full", 1.0, 0.05, 1.0, explore_tb_each, rng=rng)
            best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)
            n = int(best_arr.size)
            S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
            print(f"after base explore: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Main surrogate polishing.  These are deliberately varied:
    # pure peak, variance-flattening, active top peaks, and lower-temperature passes.
    configs = [
        dict(mode="full", tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.05, lr0=1.0, alpha=1.0,
             npeaks=0, base=0.0, seconds=260),
        dict(mode="var", tau_hi=0.5, tau_lo=0.02, lr0=1.0, alpha=2.0,
             npeaks=0, base=0.0, seconds=220),
        dict(mode="topk", tau_hi=0.5, tau_lo=0.02, lr0=1.5, alpha=1.0,
             npeaks=20, base=0.0, seconds=240),
        dict(mode="var", tau_hi=0.2, tau_lo=0.005, lr0=0.6, alpha=5.0,
             npeaks=0, base=0.0, seconds=180),
        dict(mode="topk", tau_hi=0.2, tau_lo=0.005, lr0=1.0, alpha=1.0,
             npeaks=6, base=0.0, seconds=180),
        dict(mode="full", tau_hi=0.1, tau_lo=0.003, lr0=0.35, alpha=1.0,
             npeaks=0, base=0.0, seconds=150),
    ]

    for cfg in configs:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 40:
            break
        tb = min(float(cfg["seconds"]), rem * 0.30)
        if tb < 20:
            continue

        arr, _ = run_smooth(
            best_arr, cfg["mode"], cfg["tau_hi"], cfg["tau_lo"], cfg["lr0"], tb,
            npeaks=cfg["npeaks"], base=cfg["base"], alpha=cfg["alpha"], rng=rng
        )
        best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)
        n = int(best_arr.size)
        S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
        print(f"after {cfg['mode']} pass: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Black-box smooth mutation to escape local soft-gradient minima.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    mut_tb = min(850.0, max(60.0, rem * 0.45))
    if mut_tb > 30:
        arr, _ = mutation_search(best_arr, mut_tb, rng)
        best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)
        n = int(best_arr.size)
        S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
        print(f"after mutation: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Gradient line-search around the current best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    alpha_tb = min(150.0, max(20.0, rem * 0.20))
    if alpha_tb > 15:
        arr, _ = alpha_search(best_arr, alpha_tb, rng)
        best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)
        n = int(best_arr.size)
        S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
        print(f"after alpha search: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Try length extension: often yields more room for flattening.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 260 and n <= 6000:
        ext_candidates = []
        ext_candidates.append(int(round(n * 1.25)))
        ext_candidates.append(int(round(n * 1.45)))
        for ext_n in ext_candidates:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 180:
                break
            if ext_n <= n or ext_n > 9000:
                continue
            ext0 = normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(best_arr, ext_n),
                                    float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(ext_n))))
            best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, ext0)
            if best_arr.size != ext_n:
                continue
            tb = min(130.0, rem * 0.35)
            arr, _ = run_smooth(arr, "full", 0.5, 0.02, 0.6, tb, rng=rng)
            best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)
            n = int(best_arr.size)
            S_base = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
            print(f"after extension to {ext_n}: n={n} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Final fine polishing with a very low-temperature peak pass and
    # a variance-flattening pass.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0
    if rem > 40:
        arr, _ = run_smooth(best_arr, "full", 0.08, 0.002, 0.25, rem * 0.55, rng=rng)
        best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0
        if rem > 25:
            arr, _ = run_smooth(best_arr, "var", 0.05, 0.002, 0.20, rem * 0.70,
                                alpha=3.0, rng=rng)
            best_arr, best_score = update_actual_best(best_arr, best_score, arr)

    print(f"before fallback check: n={best_arr.size} score={best_score:.6f}")

    # Verify and choose among safe fallbacks with the true evaluator.
    fallbacks = [best_arr]
    if prev is not None:
        fallbacks.append(normalize_to_sum(interpolate_seq(prev, int(best_arr.size)),
                                          float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(best_arr.size)))))
    fallbacks.append(normalize_to_sum(np.ones(int(best_arr.size), dtype=np.float64),
                                      float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(best_arr.size)))))

    final_arr = best_arr
    final_score = best_score
    for cand in fallbacks:
        final_arr, final_score = update_actual_best(final_arr, final_score, cand)

    final_arr = sanitize_sequence(final_arr)
    final_score = eval_sequence_safe(final_arr)
    print(f"final returned: n={final_arr.size} score={final_score:.6f}")

    return [float(x) for x in final_arr]
```
